# Kernel execution checklist

Updated: 24 October 2026. Current architecture focus is Phase 8 acceptance;
batch numbers group work across phases and do not replace the phase gates.

## Working agreement

Each batch delivers one reviewable outcome, lists evidence and rollback, and
updates progress.md, affected capability rows and the ignored root plan. Stop at
the batch boundary to report completed items and unresolved gates. No capability
is accepted solely because source exists or an image starts. Verification work
below requires an explicit request before adding or running test suites.

The repository owner authorized focused test execution for this ongoing refactor
on 14 October 2026. Owner-only decisions and external access needed for
remaining gates are collected in [Open Follow Ups](open-follow-ups.md);
implementation and verification continue on independent work while answers are
pending.

Remaining work is ordered in [the code-first execution plan](code-first-execution-plan.md): finish repository-owned implementation and reproducible software checks before collecting owner decisions and production inputs. Raspberry Pi hardware is unavailable, so Batch 6 uses ARM64 emulation and constrained software checks; it does not claim physical-device acceptance.

**Current snapshot (24 October 2026):** 1,075 checklist items complete and 25
open (1,100 total). The [capability matrix](capability-matrix.md) has 67 validated
rows: 24 baseline-retained, 7 deployed, and 36 prepared. Named MAN-CAP
acceptance remains separate; none is marked accepted.

### CAP-008 saved forecast Kernel read — 24 October 2026

- [x] Add a private `GET /operator/api/trends/{location_id}/forecast` read
  under exact `trend_analysis:read` and signed tenant context. It selects the
  newest saved forecast for the authorized estimate metric and returns at most
  100 numeric values and interval entries, omitting model parameters, metadata,
  IDs, and accuracy rows. Non-finite/malformed values are nulled, status is
  shown as stored, and the console labels the result advisory. Thirty-two
  focused Gateway/console/route-policy tests pass with Ruff, compilation,
  JavaScript syntax, runtime SQL inventory, matrix validation, and diff checks.
  No migration, provider, candidate, or legacy database was used. Repository
  tracing found no service writer to `time_series_forecast`, so forecast
  generation and persistence parity remain open. Rollback is a source revert
  and inventory regeneration ([contract](trend-analysis-kernel-read-contract.md);
  [evidence](builds/2026-10-24-recovery/cap-008-saved-forecast-kernel-read.json)).

### CAP-008 saved seasonal decomposition Kernel read — 24 October 2026

- [x] Add a private `GET /operator/api/trends/{location_id}/decomposition`
  read under exact `trend_analysis:read` and signed tenant context. It selects
  the latest saved decomposition for the authorized estimate metric and bounds
  each component in SQL to the first 200 aligned source-order values. The
  projection omits IDs and metadata, converts malformed components and
  non-finite values to safe empty/null values, and reports truncation. The
  hidden-by-default console validates scope and fixed component shapes,
  renders values as text, and labels truncated output partial and all output
  advisory. Twenty-seven focused Gateway/console/route-policy tests pass with
  Ruff, compilation, JavaScript syntax, runtime SQL inventory, and diff checks.
  No migration, provider, candidate, or legacy database was used. Rollback is
  a source revert and inventory regeneration. Forecast/geostatistics reads,
  populated parity, live OIDC/browser, least-privilege ACL/RLS, audience review,
  and MAN-CAP-008 remain open ([contract](trend-analysis-kernel-read-contract.md);
  [evidence](builds/2026-10-24-recovery/cap-008-seasonal-decomposition-kernel-read.json)).

### CAP-006 currency-safe energy costs — 24 October 2026

- [x] Add migration 604 with nullable `energy_reading.cost_currency_code`.
  Historical amounts remain unclassified; database checks reject malformed
  codes and require currency on new or changed cost values while allowing
  unrelated edits to historic rows. The consumption view appends the new code
  without changing its existing column order.
- [x] Require an explicit currency when the legacy energy writer records a
  rate, preserve zero-rate costs, and add `--cost-currency` to its CLI. Legacy
  consumption and cost-analysis outputs aggregate only within one currency;
  mixed or incomplete totals are withheld, with currency-specific summaries
  and unclassified counts kept visible.
- [x] Extend the signed, reviewed energy summary and operator console with
  30-day currency-separated cost totals. A comparable total appears only if
  exactly one currency is present and no classified-cost row lacks a code. The
  projection remains location-scoped and omits source names, notes, and IDs.
  Disposable PostgreSQL 16 applies/replays migration 604 and verifies legacy
  preservation, USD/DOP separation, view compatibility, malformed/missing-code
  rejection, historical update behavior, and safe unrelated edits. The 64-test
  energy Gateway/console/domain/CLI selection, Ruff, Python compilation, inline
  JavaScript syntax, runtime SQL inventory, capability validation, and diff
  checks pass. Candidate and legacy databases remain untouched; migration 604
  is not rolled out. Populated cost reconciliation, ACL/RLS, live OIDC/browser,
  and MAN-CAP-006 remain open. Rollback restores prior application code and
  retains the additive migration; no historical amount is rewritten
  ([contract](energy-monitoring-kernel-read-contract.md);
  [disposable checker](../../scripts/check-energy-reading-cost-currency-disposable.py);
  [evidence](builds/2026-10-24-recovery/cap-006-energy-cost-currency-disposable.json)).

- [x] Bind landscape habitat references to their farm. Migration 603 adds

  [tests](../../tests/test_landscape_reference_location.py);
  [checker](../../scripts/check-landscape-reference-location-disposable.py);
  [evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/cap-006-landscape-reference-location-disposable.json)).
- [x] Add an operator-run historical-reference preflight that starts a

**Phase 8 software/release update (23 October 2026):** the earlier clean
network-disabled ARM64/QEMU PostgreSQL 16 replay applied all 588 schema files
through migration 597 and cleaned up successfully
([prior evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/arm64-qemu-current-schema-597-after-bootstrap-retry.json)).
CI #771 then exposed that PostgreSQL superuser seeds were being treated as
worker operations by the lifecycle trigger. Additive migration 598 excludes
implicit superuser role membership while preserving explicit worker-role
denials; its disposable PostgreSQL 16.15 replay and authorization checks pass
([evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/agent-safety-superuser-session-disposable.json)).
Security #498 passed, but CI #774 failed seven generated worker-inventory
freshness/coverage checks after 9,114 tests and 163 subtests passed. The saved
closure, call graph, SQL inventory, SQL reachability, and boundary-preflight
reports had drifted from current sources (95 recorded graph files versus 96
generated). All five artifacts are regenerated at the current local branch;
the five focused suites pass 35 tests. The pushed repair passed CI #775 and
Security #499 ([CI repair evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/ci-774-worker-inventory-refresh.json)).
An earlier network-disabled ARM64/QEMU replay against migration 598 stopped at
migration 454 after 444 schema applications; targeted migration 454 disposable
PostgreSQL 16 acceptance passes, and that attempt remains a historical failure
([attempt](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/arm64-qemu-current-schema-598.json)). The
clean retry passed all 589 current schema files through migration 598, with zero
pending or failed files, no seeds/farm data, no host ports, runtime architecture
`aarch64`, and completed cleanup ([evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/arm64-qemu-current-schema-598-retry.json)). Its recorded source `23a206cf` is an ancestor of the current branch; no schema migration files changed afterward. This establishes current migration replay under software emulation, not physical Raspberry Pi, power, performance, or production acceptance. A repeatable focused role-boundary check now uses the digest-pinned Compose
database image and has been added to the next hosted CI batch; its local
disposable run passes all nine
assertions ([evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/agent-safety-superuser-ci-regression.json)).
The current-source ARM64 app also builds and passes a read-only, network-disabled
Gateway/SDK/job/migration/RPC smoke; physical-device and power-failure acceptance
remain open ([evidence](builds/2026-10-23-recovery/arm64-kernel-current-source-smoke-ef0bcf60.json)).

Gate: retained end-to-end evidence for the selected CAP rows, with explicit
uncovered domains. This branch checkpoint passes 77 focused migration,
workflow, service, and OIDC tests plus Ruff. Migrations 383–384 compiled and
their draft, submit, verify, reject, rework, publish, exception-close,
independent-actor, revoked-grant, content-immutability, append-only, deferred
draft-evidence, and legacy-row compatibility checks passed in disposable local
PostgreSQL ([evidence](builds/2026-10-12-recovery/farm-activity-lifecycle-isolated.json)).
The separate report/metric slice passes 11 focused tests and Ruff, including
published-only aggregation, private-field exclusion, period boundaries,
snapshot hashing/draft state, metric provenance, and unverified metric storage
([evidence](builds/2026-10-12-recovery/farm-activity-report-metric-contract.json)).
The report/metric integration also passed against the full repository schema:
the migration runner applied all 375 ordered schema migrations (schema-only, no
seeds) to a disposable PostGIS 16 database. Real service functions produced two
published activities and 5.25 hours, excluded a draft and private source marker,
stored a hash-matching unfrozen draft snapshot, and persisted a 50% rework metric
with two source records and `verified=false`. The disposable volume/network were
removed. The earlier expanded Batch 3 suite passed 91 tests and Ruff, including
wrong-location query binding/no-write checks and an injected workflow-event
failure. A second check replayed all 375 schema migrations in disposable
PostGIS 16, then used a real PostgreSQL trigger to reject the submitted
workflow event during the actual transition service call; the activity stayed
`draft`, and neither the workflow-event nor lifecycle-transition ledger
recorded `submitted`. Two simultaneous PostgreSQL promotion calls passed: one
created the draft, the other returned the same activity ID with `created=false`,
and the database retained one canonical row and one `draft_created` event. The earlier local candidate rollout applied migrations 383–384 with an exact-
target encrypted checkpoint and rollback image; OIDC remains disabled. The
canonical queue/detail, console, and migration-385 draft-edit source now pass
focused verification and are deployed to the candidate. Migration 385 has zero
pending schemas, and a fresh encrypted checkpoint restored with matching
migration fingerprints, extension versions, and workflow counts. Only the app
was recreated; PostgreSQL, Metabase, and proxy stayed healthy/running. The
candidate remains empty for farm activities, workflow events, and mobile
collections. Higher-contention stress, configured-provider OIDC, populated
workflow acceptance, and MAN-CAP-001 remain open. See [migration 383–384
rollout](builds/2026-10-12-recovery/candidate-farm-activity-rollout.json) and
[migration 385 rollout](builds/2026-10-13-recovery/candidate-farm-activity-draft-edit-rollout.json).
Migration-385 source acceptance now passes a separate 95-test focused suite plus
Ruff. A fresh disposable PostGIS 16 replay applied 376 ordered schema
migrations, then validated a synthetic draft edit, its digest-backed
append-only event, rejection of event mutation, and transaction rollback after
an injected event-insert failure. The scratch container was removed and the candidate was unchanged during this source check. See [draft-edit evidence](builds/2026-10-13-recovery/farm-activity-draft-edit-acceptance.json).

The separately authorized candidate rollout then restored a fresh encrypted checkpoint into an isolated template0 database and matched migration fingerprints, extensions, and selected workflow counts. Migration 385 applied with zero pending schemas; the app rebuilt from `f854d47` and alone was recreated. Health returned 200 and protected review, activity, and draft-edit routes returned 401 without credentials. PostgreSQL, Metabase, and proxy stayed up; the temporary restore resources were removed. See [candidate rollout evidence](builds/2026-10-13-recovery/candidate-farm-activity-draft-edit-rollout.json).

Rollback: the API release can be reverted, but migration 384's native form
guards intentionally remain active and the legacy provider must not be used to
bypass lifecycle decisions for native 1.2.0 rows. Pause intake and use a
compatible transition API or a reviewed forward fix; preserve the additive
index and event evidence.

- [x] Refresh ARM64 Kernel application and PostGIS build artifacts from the current checkout under unique local tags; retain image identities and build/runtime limitations ([record](arm64-image-pair-refresh.md)).

A local AMD64 app build completed; the ARM64 counterpart stopped during package resolution after registry DNS failed. CI run 283 exposed the missing CA bundle; [run 284](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/38449176283) confirmed the signed-snapshot CA bootstrap and built both PostGIS architectures, then the full package inventory comparison found the documented amd64-only `libquadmath0` delta. Current branch-head CI remains pending ([attempt record](builds/2026-10-13-recovery/kernel-architecture-local-build.json)).

### CAP-064 bounded EcocreditService server pagination — 21 October 2026
